Yeah, the guilt is just... it's a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those nights with the crackers and sweets, where you feel like you can't stop eating?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, it's not like a huge amount all at once. I just kind of pick at things. Like the graham crackers, I might have a few, and then maybe I'll find some leftover rice in the fridge. I actually tried making this new rice dish the other day—it had saffron in it. Saffron is so ridiculously expensive, isn't it? I saw a documentary once about how they have to harvest it by hand, like, pulling these tiny little threads out of the crocus flowers. It's fascinating. I tried growing crocuses in the front yard a couple of years ago, but the squirrels just dug up all the bulbs before they even had a chance to sprout. Squirrels are so bold around here. One of them practically walked right up to me on the porch while I was drinking my coffee. I have this favorite mug, it's shaped like an owl... but anyway, no, I wouldn't say I eat a ton of food really fast or anything. Just picking at things here and there.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers and sweets when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Stressed? Um... I guess maybe sometimes? But not always. Sometimes I'm just wandering around the kitchen because it's quiet. I like how quiet the house gets at night. You can actually hear the refrigerator humming. It's an older fridge, the kind with the freezer on the bottom. My sister has one of those fancy new ones with the screen on the door where you can look up recipes and leave notes. I don't really see the point of that, to be honest. I mean, sticky notes work just fine, don't they? I have a whole drawer full of sticky notes in different colors. Neon pink, lime green, this really pretty pastel purple... I used to use them to mark pages in my textbooks when I was taking classes at the community college. I took this art history class that was just wonderful. The professor had this very soothing voice, like he should have been reading audiobooks or something. He wore a lot of tweed. I always think tweed looks so itchy, though. Like, how do people wear that all day? But... what were we talking about? Was it about being anxious? I don't know, I just eat them because they're there, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers and sweets at night helps you feel better when you're feeling overwhelmed or upset?\n\nPatient: Feel better? Um... I don't really know. I haven't really noticed if it actually helps me feel better or not. I guess it's just something to do with my hands. Kind of like how some people knit when they're upset? I don't knit, though, I crochet. I actually made this huge afghan last winter, it was all these little granny squares in mustard yellow and teal. It took me absolute months to finish. I ended up giving it to my niece because she just moved up to Seattle for school, and you know how it is up there, it's always drizzling. I figured she'd need something warm. I actually bought her one of those umbrellas, too, the clear dome ones so you can actually see where you're walking while keeping your shoulders dry. I always hated the regular black ones, they just make everything seem so gloomy. I prefer bright colors. But... wait, what were you asking? Oh, if the sweets help. Yeah, I'm really not sure. I don't think I've ever paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating at night is related to your feelings of guilt or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Guilt? Well, I definitely feel guilty a lot of the time, but I don't know if the eating is connected to it. I just... I get stuck on things. Like, I can spend hours just replaying these tiny little things in my head. The other day, I bought this expensive tube of watercolor pigment—cerulean blue, the real stuff, not the student grade. The pigment load is just incredible, it granulates so beautifully when you do a wash. I love painting skies with it. I used to go out to the park with a little travel easel and just paint the clouds. There was this one time a golden retriever ran right through my setup and knocked my water cup everywhere. He was so happy, though, I couldn't even be mad. His owner was mortified, kept apologizing and trying to offer me money for the paper. I told him it was fine, it's just paper. They make paper out of cotton, you know, the really good watercolor paper. It holds up to all the scrubbing. He works over at the insurance company on 4th Street, the one with that big glass atrium? They just remodeled the whole lobby, and he says it echoes terribly now. My aunt has sciatica too, and she swears by this special orthopedic cushion she bought off the TV... but, um, sorry, what were we talking about? Losing control? Yeah, no, I don't feel out of control. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Internal pressure? Um... no, I don't think so. It's not like a pressure cooker or anything. I'm not sitting there feeling like I'm going to burst if I don't have a sandwich. Pressure cookers always scared me a little, to be honest. My grandmother had one of those old heavy aluminum ones that rattled on the stove. She used to make this amazing pot roast in it, with potatoes and carrots that got so soft they just melted in your mouth. She always grew her own carrots in the backyard, right next to the prize-winning tomatoes she was so proud of. She entered those tomatoes in the county fair every single year. The fair used to be so much better when I was a kid. They had this Ferris wheel that went so high you could see all the way to the water tower on the edge of town. The water tower was painted to look like a giant peach, which was weird because we didn't even grow peaches around here. Mostly just apples. There was an orchard down the road where you could pick your own... wait, what was the question? Internal pressure? Right, no. I don't feel pressured to eat. I just eat if I want to.

I don't really see the point of that, to be honest. I mean, sticky notes work just fine, don't they? I have a whole drawer full of sticky notes in different colors. Neon pink, lime green, this really pretty pastel purple... I used to use them to mark pages in my textbooks when I was taking classes at the community college. I took this art history class that was just wonderful. The professor had this very soothing voice, like he should have been reading audiobooks or something. He wore a lot of tweed. Um... I don't really know. I haven't really noticed if it actually helps me feel better or not. I guess it's just something to do with my hands. Kind of like how some people knit when they're upset? I don't knit, though, I crochet. I actually made this huge afghan last winter, it was all these little granny squares in mustard yellow and teal. It took me absolute months to finish. I ended up giving it to my niece because she just moved up to Seattle for school, and you know how it is up there, it's always drizzling. I figured she'd need something warm. I actually bought her one of those umbrellas, too, the clear dome ones so you can actually see where you're walking while keeping your shoulders dry. I don't think I've ever paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating at night is related to your feelings of guilt or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Guilt? Well, I definitely feel guilty a lot of the time, but I don't know if the eating is connected to it. I just... I get stuck on things. Like, I can spend hours just replaying these tiny little things in my head. The other day, I bought this expensive tube of watercolor pigment—cerulean blue, the real stuff, not the student grade. The pigment load is just incredible, it granulates so beautifully when you do a wash. I love painting skies with it. I used to go out to the park with a little travel easel and just paint the clouds. There was this one time a golden retriever ran right through my setup and knocked my water cup everywhere. He was so happy, though, I couldn't even be mad. His owner was mortified, kept apologizing and trying to offer me money for the paper. I told him it was fine, it's just paper. They make paper out of cotton, you know, the really good watercolor paper. It holds up to all the scrubbing. My husband loves them, even though they're probably terrible for his cholesterol. We really should be eating more vegetables. I tried growing zucchini last summer in these little raised beds out back, but the squash bugs just decimated them. It was so disappointing. Gardening is supposed to be relaxing, you know? Getting your hands in the dirt and all that. I bought these special gardening gloves that have little claws on the fingertips for digging, they look completely ridiculous, like dinosaur hands or something. But honestly, I hate going to that grocery store lately because they completely rearranged all the aisles. It makes absolutely no sense now. They put the baking supplies right next to the pet food. I like to bake when the weather gets cold, especially banana bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread, she'd put walnuts in it, which I know some people hate, but I think it gives it a really nice crunch. She had this old cast iron pan that weighed a ton. I actually have it now, it's sitting on my stove, but it's so heavy I can barely lift it to wash it. The fair used to be so much better when I was a kid. They had this Ferris wheel that went so high you could see all the way to the water tower on the edge of town. The water tower was painted to look like a giant peach, which was weird because we didn't even grow peaches around here. Mostly just apples. Worried about gaining weight? Um... well, not really worried, no. I mean, I know it can happen, but I don't sit there stressing about the scale. I actually threw my scale out a few years ago. It was one of those digital ones that always fluctuated depending on where you put it on the bathroom floor. If I moved it an inch to the left near the tub, it would say I weighed three pounds less. The tub is one of those old clawfoot ones, really deep. I love taking baths in it, especially with those lavender bath bombs my sister gets me for my birthday. She buys them from this little boutique downtown that smells so strongly of patchouli you can smell it halfway down the block. Next door to that boutique is a bakery that makes the best sourdough bread. They start the starter from this batch they've had going since like, 1990 or something crazy like that. The crust is always perfectly crispy, and if you get it right when they open, it's still warm. I used to go there every Saturday morning when I was taking classes at the community college. I was studying art history for a while, but then I realized I didn't really want to write papers about Renaissance paintings all day. The professor was this guy who wore bow ties and always cleared his throat before he made a joke... but, um, wait. What were you asking me? About weight? Oh, right. No, I don't really worry about gaining weight from eating at night. It's just not something I think about much.

Anyway... wait, what did you ask me? If I can't stop eating them? Yeah, no, I don't think it's like a compulsion or anything. No, I wouldn't call it an urge. It's not like this big, dramatic feeling where I just *have* to have it. It's just... I'm sitting there, and then I'm eating. Usually while I'm watching TV. We started rewatching that old detective show, the one set in the 70s? My husband bought the whole DVD box set at a garage sale a few weeks ago. The guy selling it had this massive collection of vintage vinyl, too. I almost bought a Fleetwood Mac album, but we don't even own a record player anymore. We used to have this beautiful wooden turntable, but it broke during our last move. The movers were so careless with some of our boxes. They completely shattered this ceramic bowl I made in a pottery class back in college. It wasn't very good, honestly, the glaze was all uneven, but I liked it. Pottery is so much harder than it looks, trying to center the clay on the wheel takes so much upper body strength. I always ended up with mud all over my shirt. But um... what were you saying? An urge? No, it's not really an urge. I just do it without thinking much about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is something you do without being able to think about not doing it?\n\nPatient: Thinking about not doing it? Um... well, I guess I could think about not doing it if I really tried. It's just that my mind is usually somewhere else entirely by that time of night. I actually tried to start keeping my hands busy instead, you know, to give myself something else to focus on. I went down to that little craft shop on Elm, the one with the faded purple awning? I bought all this chunky wool yarn because I thought I’d knit a blanket for the guest room bed. The lady working there was telling me all about how it’s spun from actual alpaca fleece. Did you know alpacas are really gentle? We took my niece to a petting zoo a few years ago for her fifth birthday, and they had this one alpaca with a funny little underbite that just kept following her around the enclosure. She was wearing this bright pink raincoat because it was drizzling that morning, and I swear the alpaca thought she was a giant piece of candy or something. My mother made me wear these awful stiff corduroy overalls to school... but, um, wait. What did you just ask me? About thinking? Yeah, I don't think it's that I *can't* think about not eating. I just... don't really think about it either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is something you do without being able to consider alternative actions?\n\nPatient: Consider alternative actions? Um... well, I suppose I could do something else if I really put my mind to it. Like, sometimes I think about just making a cup of tea instead. I bought this new herbal tea last week, chamomile and lavender. Lavender is supposed to be really relaxing, right? My neighbor, Susan, grows huge bushes of it in her front yard. It smells amazing in the summer, but honestly, her whole garden is getting a little out of hand lately. The homeowner's association actually sent her a warning letter about the weeds. They are so incredibly strict in our neighborhood. I got a notice taped to my door last year just because I left my garbage can by the curb a few hours too long after the truck came. My husband actually forgot to pick up the groceries on his way home from work on Tuesday, which was annoying because he promised he would. He works over at the insurance company on 4th Street, the one with that big glass atrium? They just remodeled the whole lobby, and he says it echoes terribly now. He was telling me about this new guy they hired who talks really loudly on the phone, and because of the echo, the whole floor can hear his conversations. Apparently, the guy is planning a trip to Hawaii and was arguing with a travel agent about rental car prices for like twenty minutes. I've never been to Hawaii. I've always wanted to see a volcano, though. I watched this documentary once about how the lava flows right into the ocean and creates all this new land. The steam it makes is just incredible, huge white clouds of it... but, um, what were we talking about? Distress? Yeah, no. If I couldn't eat, I'd probably just be slightly irritated, maybe sigh a bit, and then just watch my show. It wouldn't be a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no choice but to eat at night once you start thinking about food?\n\nPatient: No choice? Um... well, I definitely have a choice. It's not like I'm being forced or anything. I make choices all the time. Like when I go to the grocery store, I'm always making choices. I usually go to the big Safeway on the corner of 5th and Main, because the produce is just so much better there. Though, last time I went, I spent like twenty minutes just standing in the cereal aisle trying to pick out a box of cornflakes. There are just so many brands now, it's overwhelming. Do you remember when they used to make those little variety packs with the tiny cardboard boxes? You could cut them open right down the middle and pour the milk straight into the box. My brother and I used to fight over the frosted ones when we went camping. We had this huge, heavy green canvas tent that always smelled like mildew and pine needles, and it took my dad hours to set up. He'd get so frustrated with the aluminum poles because they always got stuck together in the dirt. One time he actually bent one over his knee because he was so mad at it... but, um, sorry, wait. I definitely have a choice.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you start eating at night?\n\nPatient: Losing control? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I always pay attention to the speed limit, especially on that stretch of highway right past the old paper mill. They always have a speed trap set up there behind the big billboard. He's always shouting about his \"crazy low prices.\" My uncle actually bought a truck from him once, a red pickup. It broke down two weeks later, right in the middle of an intersection. He had to get it towed all the way back to the dealership, and the tow truck driver was this really grumpy guy who complained the whole way about his sciatica. Um... no, I don't think so. It's not like a pressure cooker or anything. I'm not sitting there feeling like I'm going to burst if I don't have a sandwich. Pressure cookers always scared me a little, to be honest. My grandmother had one of those old heavy aluminum ones that rattled on the stove. She used to make this amazing pot roast in it, with potatoes and carrots that got so soft they just melted in your mouth. She always grew her own carrots in the backyard, right next to the prize-winning tomatoes she was so proud of. She entered those tomatoes in the county fair every single year. The fair used to be so much better when I was a kid. They had this Ferris wheel that went so high you could see all the way to the water tower on the edge of town. There was an orchard down the road where you could pick your own... wait, what was the question? Internal pressure? Right, no. I don't feel pressured to eat. I just eat if I want to.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight when you eat at night?\n\nPatient: Worried about gaining weight? Um... well, not really worried, no. I mean, I know it can happen, but I don't sit there stressing about the scale. I actually threw my scale out a few years ago. The tub is one of those old clawfoot ones, really deep. I love taking baths in it, especially with those lavender bath bombs my sister gets me for my birthday. She buys them from this little boutique downtown that smells so strongly of patchouli you can smell it halfway down the block. Next door to that boutique is a bakery that makes the best sourdough bread. They start the starter from this batch they've had going since like, 1990 or something crazy like that. The crust is always perfectly crispy, and if you get it right when they open, it's still warm. I used to go there every Saturday morning when I was taking classes at the community college. The professor was this guy who wore bow ties and always cleared his throat before he made a joke... but, um, wait. What were you asking me? About weight? Oh, right. No, I don't really worry about gaining weight from eating at night. It's just not something I think about much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Comfort myself? Um... well, maybe a little? But it's more that my eating has just been all over the place lately, which is actually what finally pushed me to come in and get help. Like, some weeks I barely touch my dinner, just move it around the plate. But then other nights, I'll just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. The kitchen is always so quiet at night, except for the hum of the refrigerator. It's an older model, one of those side-by-sides with the ice maker in the door. The ice maker actually broke last summer right in the middle of that huge heatwave we had. It was miserable. We had to buy bags of ice from the gas station on the corner. The guy who works the night shift there always wears these really bright, neon-colored shirts. He said his daughter bought them for him because he used to ride his bike to work and she wanted him to be visible to cars. Biking at night always seemed dangerous to me, especially since the streetlights in our neighborhood are so dim. The city said they were going to replace them with LED bulbs, but they only did half the street and then just stopped. It looks so strange, half bright white and half that weird orange glow. The orange glow kind of reminds me of Halloween, which used to be my favorite holiday until... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the eating. Yeah, it's just very unpredictable right now, the crackers and the sweets and not eating dinner. It's just all over the place.

The cashier at the grocery store—she had this gorgeous curly red hair, it totally reminded me of my old roommate from back when I was taking some classes at the community college. We used to stay up late too, but that was different, you know? We'd watch those late-night infomercials. My eating habits are just really messy right now, and I guess that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating those crackers and sweets at night?\n\nPatient: Control? Um... I'm not really sure if it's about control. I mean, I'm just standing there in the dark chewing, mostly. I guess I just get caught up in my thoughts. Like, the other night I was eating them and looking out the window over the sink, and I saw Mr. Henderson's cat across the street. He has this huge orange tabby that always sits right on top of his mailbox. Mailboxes are kind of weird now, aren't they? Like, the metal ones get so incredibly hot in the summer. I actually burned my hand on one when I was a little girl. We lived in this house with a bright yellow door back then. My mom just loved yellow. She painted our entire kitchen yellow, too, which honestly was a bit much when you're trying to wake up early in the morning for school. She used to make me eggs every single day... But yeah, the cat was just sitting there staring at the streetlamp. Wait, what were you asking me? Something about the kitchen?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating those crackers and sweets at night?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh... yeah. Yeah, I do. It's not just the crackers, though. I feel guilty about almost everything. I can spend hours just sitting there, replaying these tiny little things in my head. Like... the other day I bought this really expensive tube of cerulean blue pigment. I like to paint sometimes, mostly water-colors, though I haven't done much of it lately. My aunt used to paint. She did these beautiful landscapes of the coast, up in Maine, I think. Or maybe it was Oregon. Anyway, the rocks were always so jagged and grey. I prefer painting flowers, mostly. But yeah, I bought that paint, or like, if I snap at my spouse over something stupid, I just spiral. I start feeling like I've completely ruined the whole household, just because I bought one tube of paint or got a little irritable. It's exhausting. My spouse is usually pretty patient, though. They like to garden. We have these huge tomato plants out back that are just taking over everything. I don't even really like tomatoes, honestly. The texture is just too squishy. But... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, the guilt is just... it's a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those nights with the crackers and sweets, where you feel like you can't stop eating?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, it's not like a huge amount all at once. I just kind of pick at things. Like the graham crackers, I might have a few, and then maybe I'll find some leftover rice in the fridge. I actually tried making this new rice dish the other day—it had saffron in it. Saffron is so ridiculously expensive, isn't it? I saw a documentary once about how they have to harvest it by hand, like, pulling these tiny little threads out of the crocus flowers. It's fascinating. I tried growing crocuses in the front yard a couple of years ago, but the squirrels just dug up all the bulbs before they even had a chance to sprout. Squirrels are so bold around here. One of them practically walked right up to me on the porch while I was drinking my coffee. I have this favorite mug, it's shaped like an owl... but anyway, no, I wouldn't say I eat a ton of food really fast or anything. Just picking at things here and there.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers and sweets when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Stressed? Um... I guess maybe sometimes? But not always. Sometimes I'm just wandering around the kitchen because it's quiet. I like how quiet the house gets at night. You can actually hear the refrigerator humming. It's an older fridge, the kind with the freezer on the bottom. My sister has one of those fancy new ones with the screen on the door where you can look up recipes and leave notes. I don't really see the point of that, to be honest. I mean, sticky notes work just fine, don't they? I have a whole drawer full of sticky notes in different colors. Neon pink, lime green, this really pretty pastel purple... I used to use them to mark pages in my textbooks when I was taking classes at the community college. I took this art history class that was just wonderful. The professor had this very soothing voice, like he should have been reading audiobooks or something. He wore a lot of tweed. I always think tweed looks so itchy, though. Like, how do people wear that all day? But... what were we talking about? Was it about being anxious? I don't know, I just eat them because they're there, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers and sweets at night helps you feel better when you're feeling overwhelmed or upset?\n\nPatient: Feel better? Um... I don't really know. I haven't really noticed if it actually helps me feel better or not. I guess it's just something to do with my hands. Kind of like how some people knit when they're upset? I don't knit, though, I crochet. I actually made this huge afghan last winter, it was all these little granny squares in mustard yellow and teal. It took me absolute months to finish. I ended up giving it to my niece because she just moved up to Seattle for school, and you know how it is up there, it's always drizzling. I figured she'd need something warm. I actually bought her one of those umbrellas, too, the clear dome ones so you can actually see where you're walking while keeping your shoulders dry. I always hated the regular black ones, they just make everything seem so gloomy. I prefer bright colors. But... wait, what were you asking? Oh, if the sweets help. Yeah, I'm really not sure. I don't think I've ever paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating at night is related to your feelings of guilt or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Guilt? Well, I definitely feel guilty a lot of the time, but I don't know if the eating is connected to it. I just... I get stuck on things. Like, I can spend hours just replaying these tiny little things in my head. The other day, I bought this expensive tube of watercolor pigment—cerulean blue, the real stuff, not the student grade. The pigment load is just incredible, it granulates so beautifully when you do a wash. I love painting skies with it. I used to go out to the park with a little travel easel and just paint the clouds. There was this one time a golden retriever ran right through my setup and knocked my water cup everywhere. He was so happy, though, I couldn't even be mad. His owner was mortified, kept apologizing and trying to offer me money for the paper. I told him it was fine, it's just paper. They make paper out of cotton, you know, the really good watercolor paper. It holds up to all the scrubbing. But anyway, I bought that paint, or like, if I snap at my husband over something stupid, I just... I sit there and feel like I've completely ruined the whole household because of it. It's this heavy feeling. But the eating... I don't know if that's why I'm in the kitchen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating at night helps you cope with your feelings of guilt or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Cope with it? Um... I don't really know if it's coping. It's just sort of... what happens. Like I said, I just feel really heavy, and then I'm in the kitchen. The kitchen is always the warmest room in the house, especially if the oven's been on. I made a casserole the other night, one of those tater tot ones? My husband loves them, even though they're probably terrible for his cholesterol. We really should be eating more vegetables. I tried growing zucchini last summer in these little raised beds out back, but the squash bugs just decimated them. It was so disappointing. Gardening is supposed to be relaxing, you know? Getting your hands in the dirt and all that. I bought these special gardening gloves that have little claws on the fingertips for digging, they look completely ridiculous, like dinosaur hands or something. I wore them once and the neighbor's cat just stared at me like I was crazy. He's this big orange tabby, always sitting on our fence. I think his name is Marmalade. But... what did you ask? About coping? Yeah, I just... I don't know if the eating really does anything for the guilt. I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to eat those crackers and sweets at night, like you can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: Can't stop myself? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. It's not like I'm out of control or anything, I just... I don't really think about stopping. I just eat them. I usually get those buttery round ones, you know, from the store on 4th street? But honestly, I hate going to that grocery store lately because they completely rearranged all the aisles. It makes absolutely no sense now. They put the baking supplies right next to the pet food. Why would you put flour next to cat litter? It's just bizarre. I spent twenty minutes last Tuesday just looking for vanilla extract. I like to bake when the weather gets cold, especially banana bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread, she'd put walnuts in it, which I know some people hate, but I think it gives it a really nice crunch. She had this old cast iron pan that weighed a ton. I actually have it now, it's sitting on my stove, but it's so heavy I can barely lift it to wash it. You really have to season those things properly with oil or they rust almost instantly. I tried using flaxseed oil once because I read online it was the best, but it made the whole house smell funny. Anyway... wait, what did you ask me? If I can't stop eating them? Yeah, no, I don't think it's like a compulsion or anything. I just haven't really tried to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating at night because you feel an overwhelming urge to do so?\n\nPatient: An overwhelming urge? No, I wouldn't call it an urge. It's not like this big, dramatic feeling where I just *have* to have it. It's just... I'm sitting there, and then I'm eating. Usually while I'm watching TV. We started rewatching that old detective show, the one set in the 70s? My husband bought the whole DVD box set at a garage sale a few weeks ago. The guy selling it had this massive collection of vintage vinyl, too. I almost bought a Fleetwood Mac album, but we don't even own a record player anymore. We used to have this beautiful wooden turntable, but it broke during our last move. The movers were so careless with some of our boxes. They completely shattered this ceramic bowl I made in a pottery class back in college. It wasn't very good, honestly, the glaze was all uneven, but I liked it. Pottery is so much harder than it looks, trying to center the clay on the wheel takes so much upper body strength. I always ended up with mud all over my shirt. But um... what were you saying? An urge? No, it's not really an urge. I just do it without thinking much about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is something you do without being able to think about not doing it?\n\nPatient: Thinking about not doing it? Um... well, I guess I could think about not doing it if I really tried. It's just that my mind is usually somewhere else entirely by that time of night. I actually tried to start keeping my hands busy instead, you know, to give myself something else to focus on. I went down to that little craft shop on Elm, the one with the faded purple awning? I bought all this chunky wool yarn because I thought I’d knit a blanket for the guest room bed. The lady working there was telling me all about how it’s spun from actual alpaca fleece. Did you know alpacas are really gentle? We took my niece to a petting zoo a few years ago for her fifth birthday, and they had this one alpaca with a funny little underbite that just kept following her around the enclosure. She was wearing this bright pink raincoat because it was drizzling that morning, and I swear the alpaca thought she was a giant piece of candy or something. Kids' clothes are so incredibly bright these days, not like when I was little. Everything I wore in the seventies was brown or mustard yellow. My mother made me wear these awful stiff corduroy overalls to school... but, um, wait. What did you just ask me? About thinking? Yeah, I don't think it's that I *can't* think about not eating. I just... don't really think about it either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is something you do without being able to consider alternative actions?\n\nPatient: Consider alternative actions? Um... well, I suppose I could do something else if I really put my mind to it. Like, sometimes I think about just making a cup of tea instead. I bought this new herbal tea last week, chamomile and lavender. Lavender is supposed to be really relaxing, right? My neighbor, Susan, grows huge bushes of it in her front yard. It smells amazing in the summer, but honestly, her whole garden is getting a little out of hand lately. The homeowner's association actually sent her a warning letter about the weeds. They are so incredibly strict in our neighborhood. I got a notice taped to my door last year just because I left my garbage can by the curb a few hours too long after the truck came. It's ridiculous, the guy who drives the little neighborhood patrol car is always just creeping around looking for violations. He drives this little golf cart thing with a flashing yellow light on top, and it beeps so loudly whenever he puts it in reverse. It always wakes up the neighbor's dog... but, um, wait. What was the question? Something about alternatives? Yeah, I don't think I'm unable to consider them. I just... don't. I just end up eating the crackers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would experience significant distress if you weren't able to eat at night?\n\nPatient: Significant distress? Um... no, I wouldn't say I'd be distressed. It's not like I'd panic or anything. If we were out of snacks, I'd probably just be a little annoyed. My husband actually forgot to pick up the groceries on his way home from work on Tuesday, which was annoying because he promised he would. He works over at the insurance company on 4th Street, the one with that big glass atrium? They just remodeled the whole lobby, and he says it echoes terribly now. He was telling me about this new guy they hired who talks really loudly on the phone, and because of the echo, the whole floor can hear his conversations. Apparently, the guy is planning a trip to Hawaii and was arguing with a travel agent about rental car prices for like twenty minutes. I've never been to Hawaii. I've always wanted to see a volcano, though. I watched this documentary once about how the lava flows right into the ocean and creates all this new land. The steam it makes is just incredible, huge white clouds of it... but, um, what were we talking about? Distress? Yeah, no. If I couldn't eat, I'd probably just be slightly irritated, maybe sigh a bit, and then just watch my show. It wouldn't be a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no choice but to eat at night once you start thinking about food?\n\nPatient: No choice? Um... well, I definitely have a choice. It's not like I'm being forced or anything. I make choices all the time. Like when I go to the grocery store, I'm always making choices. I usually go to the big Safeway on the corner of 5th and Main, because the produce is just so much better there. Though, last time I went, I spent like twenty minutes just standing in the cereal aisle trying to pick out a box of cornflakes. There are just so many brands now, it's overwhelming. Do you remember when they used to make those little variety packs with the tiny cardboard boxes? You could cut them open right down the middle and pour the milk straight into the box. My brother and I used to fight over the frosted ones when we went camping. We had this huge, heavy green canvas tent that always smelled like mildew and pine needles, and it took my dad hours to set up. He'd get so frustrated with the aluminum poles because they always got stuck together in the dirt. One time he actually bent one over his knee because he was so mad at it... but, um, sorry, wait. What did you ask me? About having a choice? Right, yeah. No, I don't feel forced to eat. If I think about food, I can just choose not to have anything. I definitely have a choice.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you start eating at night?\n\nPatient: Losing control? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing. It's not like I just black out and wake up with crumbs everywhere. I'm very aware. Just like I'm aware when I'm driving, you know? I always pay attention to the speed limit, especially on that stretch of highway right past the old paper mill. They always have a speed trap set up there behind the big billboard. The billboard used to advertise that local car dealership, the one with the guy who wears the giant cowboy hat in all the commercials? He's always shouting about his \"crazy low prices.\" My uncle actually bought a truck from him once, a red pickup. It broke down two weeks later, right in the middle of an intersection. He had to get it towed all the way back to the dealership, and the tow truck driver was this really grumpy guy who complained the whole way about his sciatica. My aunt has sciatica too, and she swears by this special orthopedic cushion she bought off the TV... but, um, sorry, what were we talking about? Losing control? Yeah, no, I don't feel out of control. I just eat the food. I'm completely in charge of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating at night because you feel an internal pressure to do so, even if you're not really hungry?\n\nPatient: Internal pressure? Um... no, I don't think so. It's not like a pressure cooker or anything. I'm not sitting there feeling like I'm going to burst if I don't have a sandwich. Pressure cookers always scared me a little, to be honest. My grandmother had one of those old heavy aluminum ones that rattled on the stove. She used to make this amazing pot roast in it, with potatoes and carrots that got so soft they just melted in your mouth. She always grew her own carrots in the backyard, right next to the prize-winning tomatoes she was so proud of. She entered those tomatoes in the county fair every single year. The fair used to be so much better when I was a kid. They had this Ferris wheel that went so high you could see all the way to the water tower on the edge of town. The water tower was painted to look like a giant peach, which was weird because we didn't even grow peaches around here. Mostly just apples. There was an orchard down the road where you could pick your own... wait, what was the question? Internal pressure? Right, no. I don't feel pressured to eat. I just eat if I want to.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight when you eat at night?\n\nPatient: Worried about gaining weight? Um... well, not really worried, no. I mean, I know it can happen, but I don't sit there stressing about the scale. I actually threw my scale out a few years ago. It was one of those digital ones that always fluctuated depending on where you put it on the bathroom floor. If I moved it an inch to the left near the tub, it would say I weighed three pounds less. The tub is one of those old clawfoot ones, really deep. I love taking baths in it, especially with those lavender bath bombs my sister gets me for my birthday. She buys them from this little boutique downtown that smells so strongly of patchouli you can smell it halfway down the block. Next door to that boutique is a bakery that makes the best sourdough bread. They start the starter from this batch they've had going since like, 1990 or something crazy like that. The crust is always perfectly crispy, and if you get it right when they open, it's still warm. I used to go there every Saturday morning when I was taking classes at the community college. I was studying art history for a while, but then I realized I didn't really want to write papers about Renaissance paintings all day. The professor was this guy who wore bow ties and always cleared his throat before he made a joke... but, um, wait. What were you asking me? About weight? Oh, right. No, I don't really worry about gaining weight from eating at night. It's just not something I think about much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat at night because you're trying to comfort yourself from feeling upset or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Comfort myself? Um... well, maybe a little? But it's more that my eating has just been all over the place lately, which is actually what finally pushed me to come in and get help. Like, some weeks I barely touch my dinner, just move it around the plate. But then other nights, I'll just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. The kitchen is always so quiet at night, except for the hum of the refrigerator. It's an older model, one of those side-by-sides with the ice maker in the door. The ice maker actually broke last summer right in the middle of that huge heatwave we had. It was miserable. We had to buy bags of ice from the gas station on the corner. The guy who works the night shift there always wears these really bright, neon-colored shirts. He said his daughter bought them for him because he used to ride his bike to work and she wanted him to be visible to cars. Biking at night always seemed dangerous to me, especially since the streetlights in our neighborhood are so dim. The city said they were going to replace them with LED bulbs, but they only did half the street and then just stopped. It looks so strange, half bright white and half that weird orange glow. The orange glow kind of reminds me of Halloween, which used to be my favorite holiday until... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the eating. Yeah, it's just very unpredictable right now, the crackers and the sweets and not eating dinner. It's just all over the place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The steam it makes is just incredible, huge white clouds of it... but, um, what were we talking about? Distress? Yeah, no. If I couldn't eat, I'd probably just be slightly irritated, maybe sigh a bit, and then just watch my show. It wouldn't be a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no choice but to eat at night once you start thinking about food?\n\nPatient: No choice? Um... well, I definitely have a choice. It's not like I'm being forced or anything. I make choices all the time. Like when I go to the grocery store, I'm always making choices. I usually go to the big Safeway on the corner of 5th and Main, because the produce is just so much better there. Though, last time I went, I spent like twenty minutes just standing in the cereal aisle trying to pick out a box of cornflakes. There are just so many brands now, it's overwhelming. Do you remember when they used to make those little variety packs with the tiny cardboard boxes? You could cut them open right down the middle and pour the milk straight into the box. My brother and I used to fight over the frosted ones when we went camping. We had this huge, heavy green canvas tent that always smelled like mildew and pine needles, and it took my dad hours to set up. He'd get so frustrated with the aluminum poles because they always got stuck together in the dirt. One time he actually bent one over his knee because he was so mad at it... but, um, sorry, wait. What did you ask me? About having a choice? Right, yeah. No, I don't feel forced to eat. If I think about food, I can just choose not to have anything. I definitely have a choice.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you start eating at night?\n\nPatient: Losing control? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I know exactly what I'm doing. It's not like I just black out and wake up with crumbs everywhere. I'm very aware. Just like I'm aware when I'm driving, you know? I always pay attention to the speed limit, especially on that stretch of highway right past the old paper mill. They always have a speed trap set up there behind the big billboard. The billboard used to advertise that local car dealership, the one with the guy who wears the giant cowboy hat in all the commercials? He's always shouting about his \"crazy low prices.\" My uncle actually bought a truck from him once, a red pickup. It broke down two weeks later, right in the middle of an intersection. He had to get it towed all the way back to the dealership, and the tow truck driver was this really grumpy guy who complained the whole way about his sciatica. My aunt has sciatica too, and she swears by this special orthopedic cushion she bought off the TV... but, um, sorry, what were we talking about? Losing control? Yeah, no, I don't feel out of control. I just eat the food. I'm completely in charge of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating at night because you feel an internal pressure to do so, even if you're not really hungry?\n\nPatient: Internal pressure? Um... no, I don't think so. It's not like a pressure cooker or anything. I'm not sitting there feeling like I'm going to burst if I don't have a sandwich. Pressure cookers always scared me a little, to be honest. My grandmother had one of those old heavy aluminum ones that rattled on the stove. She used to make this amazing pot roast in it, with potatoes and carrots that got so soft they just melted in your mouth. She always grew her own carrots in the backyard, right next to the prize-winning tomatoes she was so proud of. She entered those tomatoes in the county fair every single year. The fair used to be so much better when I was a kid. They had this Ferris wheel that went so high you could see all the way to the water tower on the edge of town. The water tower was painted to look like a giant peach, which was weird because we didn't even grow peaches around here. Mostly just apples. There was an orchard down the road where you could pick your own... wait, what was the question? Internal pressure? Right, no. I don't feel pressured to eat. I just eat if I want to.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight when you eat at night?\n\nPatient: Worried about gaining weight? Um... well, not really worried, no. I mean, I know it can happen, but I don't sit there stressing about the scale. I actually threw my scale out a few years ago. It was one of those digital ones that always fluctuated depending on where you put it on the bathroom floor. If I moved it an inch to the left near the tub, it would say I weighed three pounds less. The tub is one of those old clawfoot ones, really deep. I love taking baths in it, especially with those lavender bath bombs my sister gets me for my birthday. She buys them from this little boutique downtown that smells so strongly of patchouli you can smell it halfway down the block. Next door to that boutique is a bakery that makes the best sourdough bread. They start the starter from this batch they've had going since like, 1990 or something crazy like that. The crust is always perfectly crispy, and if you get it right when they open, it's still warm. I used to go there every Saturday morning when I was taking classes at the community college. I was studying art history for a while, but then I realized I didn't really want to write papers about Renaissance paintings all day. The professor was this guy who wore bow ties and always cleared his throat before he made a joke... but, um, wait. What were you asking me? About weight? Oh, right. No, I don't really worry about gaining weight from eating at night. It's just not something I think about much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat at night because you're trying to comfort yourself from feeling upset or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Comfort myself? Um... well, maybe a little? But it's more that my eating has just been all over the place lately, which is actually what finally pushed me to come in and get help. Like, some weeks I barely touch my dinner, just move it around the plate. But then other nights, I'll just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. The kitchen is always so quiet at night, except for the hum of the refrigerator. It's an older model, one of those side-by-sides with the ice maker in the door. The ice maker actually broke last summer right in the middle of that huge heatwave we had. It was miserable. We had to buy bags of ice from the gas station on the corner. The guy who works the night shift there always wears these really bright, neon-colored shirts. He said his daughter bought them for him because he used to ride his bike to work and she wanted him to be visible to cars. Biking at night always seemed dangerous to me, especially since the streetlights in our neighborhood are so dim. The city said they were going to replace them with LED bulbs, but they only did half the street and then just stopped. It looks so strange, half bright white and half that weird orange glow. The orange glow kind of reminds me of Halloween, which used to be my favorite holiday until... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the eating. Yeah, it's just very unpredictable right now, the crackers and the sweets and not eating dinner. It's just all over the place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Feeling emotional? Um... I don't know if I'd say I'm eating because of my feelings. It's more like... I don't know, maybe I'm just bored sometimes? Or I just see the cookies sitting there on the counter. I bought this really nice ceramic cookie jar at a garage sale a few weeks ago. It's shaped like a little owl, with big painted eyes. The lady who sold it to me said it belonged to her mother, who collected all sorts of owl things. She had owl figurines, owl salt and pepper shakers, even a clock that hooted on the hour instead of chiming. Clocks like that always kind of startle me, to be honest. My grandfather had a cuckoo clock that drove me crazy when I'd spend the night at his house. It was right outside the guest bedroom, and every hour, this little wooden bird would pop out and just... squawk. He brought it back from Germany when he was stationed there in the army. He used to tell the best stories about being in Europe, how different the food was and the architecture. He loved the old cathedrals with the stained glass windows. Stained glass is so beautiful when the light hits it just right, especially in the late afternoon when the sun is setting and it casts all those colors across the floor... um, wait. What were you asking me? About feelings? Oh. No, I don't think I'm eating because I'm emotional. I just... I don't really know why I do it.

We used to stay up late too, but that was different, you know? We'd watch those late-night infomercials. Anyway, the cashier told me about the sale, so I bought them, even though I don't usually buy sweets like that. I just can't pass up a good bargain. But yeah... what was I saying? Oh, right. My eating habits are just really messy right now, and I guess that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating those crackers and sweets at night?\n\nPatient: Control? Um... I'm not really sure if it's about control. I mean, I'm just standing there in the dark chewing, mostly. I guess I just get caught up in my thoughts. Like, the other night I was eating them and looking out the window over the sink, and I saw Mr. Henderson's cat across the street. He has this huge orange tabby that always sits right on top of his mailbox. Mailboxes are kind of weird now, aren't they? Like, the metal ones get so incredibly hot in the summer. I actually burned my hand on one when I was a little girl. We lived in this house with a bright yellow door back then. My mom just loved yellow. She painted our entire kitchen yellow, too, which honestly was a bit much when you're trying to wake up early in the morning for school. She used to make me eggs every single day... But yeah, the cat was just sitting there staring at the streetlamp. Wait, what were you asking me? Something about the kitchen?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating those crackers and sweets at night?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh... yeah. Yeah, I do. It's not just the crackers, though. I feel guilty about almost everything. I can spend hours just sitting there, replaying these tiny little things in my head. Like... the other day I bought this really expensive tube of cerulean blue pigment. I like to paint sometimes, mostly water-colors, though I haven't done much of it lately. My aunt used to paint. She did these beautiful landscapes of the coast, up in Maine, I think. Or maybe it was Oregon. Anyway, the rocks were always so jagged and grey. I prefer painting flowers, mostly. But yeah, I bought that paint, or like, if I snap at my spouse over something stupid, I just spiral. I start feeling like I've completely ruined the whole household, just because I bought one tube of paint or got a little irritable. It's exhausting. My spouse is usually pretty patient, though. They like to garden. We have these huge tomato plants out back that are just taking over everything. I don't even really like tomatoes, honestly. The texture is just too squishy. But... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, the guilt is just... it's a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those nights with the crackers and sweets, where you feel like you can't stop eating?\n\nPatient: I don't think so? I mean, it's not like a huge amount all at once. I just kind of pick at things. Like the graham crackers, I might have a few, and then maybe I'll find some leftover rice in the fridge. I actually tried making this new rice dish the other day—it had saffron in it. Saffron is so ridiculously expensive, isn't it? I saw a documentary once about how they have to harvest it by hand, like, pulling these tiny little threads out of the crocus flowers. It's fascinating. I tried growing crocuses in the front yard a couple of years ago, but the squirrels just dug up all the bulbs before they even had a chance to sprout. Squirrels are so bold around here. One of them practically walked right up to me on the porch while I was drinking my coffee. I have this favorite mug, it's shaped like an owl... but anyway, no, I wouldn't say I eat a ton of food really fast or anything. Just picking at things here and there.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers and sweets when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Stressed? Um... I guess maybe sometimes? But not always. Sometimes I'm just wandering around the kitchen because it's quiet. I like how quiet the house gets at night. You can actually hear the refrigerator humming. It's an older fridge, the kind with the freezer on the bottom. My sister has one of those fancy new ones with the screen on the door where you can look up recipes and leave notes. I don't really see the point of that, to be honest. I mean, sticky notes work just fine, don't they? I have a whole drawer full of sticky notes in different colors. Neon pink, lime green, this really pretty pastel purple... I used to use them to mark pages in my textbooks when I was taking classes at the community college. I took this art history class that was just wonderful. The professor had this very soothing voice, like he should have been reading audiobooks or something. He wore a lot of tweed. I always think tweed looks so itchy, though. Like, how do people wear that all day? But... what were we talking about? Was it about being anxious? I don't know, I just eat them because they're there, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers and sweets at night helps you feel better when you're feeling overwhelmed or upset?\n\nPatient: Feel better? Um... I don't really know. I haven't really noticed if it actually helps me feel better or not. I guess it's just something to do with my hands. Kind of like how some people knit when they're upset? I don't knit, though, I crochet. I actually made this huge afghan last winter, it was all these little granny squares in mustard yellow and teal. It took me absolute months to finish. I ended up giving it to my niece because she just moved up to Seattle for school, and you know how it is up there, it's always drizzling. I figured she'd need something warm. I actually bought her one of those umbrellas, too, the clear dome ones so you can actually see where you're walking while keeping your shoulders dry. I always hated the regular black ones, they just make everything seem so gloomy. I prefer bright colors. But... wait, what were you asking? Oh, if the sweets help. Yeah, I'm really not sure. I don't think I've ever paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating at night is related to your feelings of guilt or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Guilt? Well, I definitely feel guilty a lot of the time, but I don't know if the eating is connected to it. I just... I get stuck on things. Like, I can spend hours just replaying these tiny little things in my head. The other day, I bought this expensive tube of watercolor pigment—cerulean blue, the real stuff, not the student grade. The pigment load is just incredible, it granulates so beautifully when you do a wash. I love painting skies with it. I used to go out to the park with a little travel easel and just paint the clouds. There was this one time a golden retriever ran right through my setup and knocked my water cup everywhere. He was so happy, though, I couldn't even be mad. His owner was mortified, kept apologizing and trying to offer me money for the paper. I told him it was fine, it's just paper. They make paper out of cotton, you know, the really good watercolor paper. It holds up to all the scrubbing. But anyway, I bought that paint, or like, if I snap at my husband over something stupid, I just... I sit there and feel like I've completely ruined the whole household because of it. It's this heavy feeling. But the eating... I don't know if that's why I'm in the kitchen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating at night helps you cope with your feelings of guilt or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Cope with it? Um... I don't really know if it's coping. It's just sort of... what happens. Like I said, I just feel really heavy, and then I'm in the kitchen. The kitchen is always the warmest room in the house, especially if the oven's been on. I made a casserole the other night, one of those tater tot ones? My husband loves them, even though they're probably terrible for his cholesterol. We really should be eating more vegetables. I tried growing zucchini last summer in these little raised beds out back, but the squash bugs just decimated them. It was so disappointing. Gardening is supposed to be relaxing, you know? Getting your hands in the dirt and all that. I bought these special gardening gloves that have little claws on the fingertips for digging, they look completely ridiculous, like dinosaur hands or something. I wore them once and the neighbor's cat just stared at me like I was crazy. He's this big orange tabby, always sitting on our fence. I think his name is Marmalade. But... what did you ask? About coping? Yeah, I just... I don't know if the eating really does anything for the guilt. I just do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to eat those crackers and sweets at night, like you can't stop yourself?\n\nPatient: Can't stop myself? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. It's not like I'm out of control or anything, I just... I don't really think about stopping. I just eat them. I usually get those buttery round ones, you know, from the store on 4th street? But honestly, I hate going to that grocery store lately because they completely rearranged all the aisles. It makes absolutely no sense now. They put the baking supplies right next to the pet food. Why would you put flour next to cat litter? It's just bizarre. I spent twenty minutes last Tuesday just looking for vanilla extract. I like to bake when the weather gets cold, especially banana bread. My grandmother used to make the best banana bread, she'd put walnuts in it, which I know some people hate, but I think it gives it a really nice crunch. She had this old cast iron pan that weighed a ton. I actually have it now, it's sitting on my stove, but it's so heavy I can barely lift it to wash it. You really have to season those things properly with oil or they rust almost instantly. I tried using flaxseed oil once because I read online it was the best, but it made the whole house smell funny. Anyway... wait, what did you ask me? If I can't stop eating them? Yeah, no, I don't think it's like a compulsion or anything. I just haven't really tried to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating at night because you feel an overwhelming urge to do so?\n\nPatient: An overwhelming urge? No, I wouldn't call it an urge. It's not like this big, dramatic feeling where I just *have* to have it. It's just... I'm sitting there, and then I'm eating. Usually while I'm watching TV. We started rewatching that old detective show, the one set in the 70s? My husband bought the whole DVD box set at a garage sale a few weeks ago. The guy selling it had this massive collection of vintage vinyl, too. I almost bought a Fleetwood Mac album, but we don't even own a record player anymore. We used to have this beautiful wooden turntable, but it broke during our last move. The movers were so careless with some of our boxes. They completely shattered this ceramic bowl I made in a pottery class back in college. It wasn't very good, honestly, the glaze was all uneven, but I liked it. Pottery is so much harder than it looks, trying to center the clay on the wheel takes so much upper body strength. I always ended up with mud all over my shirt. But um... what were you saying? An urge? No, it's not really an urge. I just do it without thinking much about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is something you do without being able to think about not doing it?\n\nPatient: Thinking about not doing it? Um... well, I guess I could think about not doing it if I really tried. It's just that my mind is usually somewhere else entirely by that time of night. I actually tried to start keeping my hands busy instead, you know, to give myself something else to focus on. I went down to that little craft shop on Elm, the one with the faded purple awning? I bought all this chunky wool yarn because I thought I’d knit a blanket for the guest room bed. The lady working there was telling me all about how it’s spun from actual alpaca fleece. Did you know alpacas are really gentle? We took my niece to a petting zoo a few years ago for her fifth birthday, and they had this one alpaca with a funny little underbite that just kept following her around the enclosure. She was wearing this bright pink raincoat because it was drizzling that morning, and I swear the alpaca thought she was a giant piece of candy or something. Kids' clothes are so incredibly bright these days, not like when I was little. Everything I wore in the seventies was brown or mustard yellow. My mother made me wear these awful stiff corduroy overalls to school... but, um, wait. What did you just ask me? About thinking? Yeah, I don't think it's that I *can't* think about not eating. I just... don't really think about it either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating at night is something you do without being able to consider alternative actions?\n\nPatient: Consider alternative actions? Um... well, I suppose I could do something else if I really put my mind to it. Like, sometimes I think about just making a cup of tea instead. I bought this new herbal tea last week, chamomile and lavender. Lavender is supposed to be really relaxing, right? My neighbor, Susan, grows huge bushes of it in her front yard. It smells amazing in the summer, but honestly, her whole garden is getting a little out of hand lately. The homeowner's association actually sent her a warning letter about the weeds. They are so incredibly strict in our neighborhood. I got a notice taped to my door last year just because I left my garbage can by the curb a few hours too long after the truck came. It's ridiculous, the guy who drives the little neighborhood patrol car is always just creeping around looking for violations. He drives this little golf cart thing with a flashing yellow light on top, and it beeps so loudly whenever he puts it in reverse. It always wakes up the neighbor's dog... but, um, wait. I just end up eating the crackers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would experience significant distress if you weren't able to eat at night?\n\nPatient: Significant distress? Um... no, I wouldn't say I'd be distressed. It's not like I'd panic or anything. If we were out of snacks, I'd probably just be a little annoyed. My husband actually forgot to pick up the groceries on his way home from work on Tuesday, which was annoying because he promised he would. He works over at the insurance company on 4th Street, the one with that big glass atrium? They just remodeled the whole lobby, and he says it echoes terribly now. He was telling me about this new guy they hired who talks really loudly on the phone, and because of the echo, the whole floor can hear his conversations. Apparently, the guy is planning a trip to Hawaii and was arguing with a travel agent about rental car prices for like twenty minutes. I've never been to Hawaii. I've always wanted to see a volcano, though. I watched this documentary once about how the lava flows right into the ocean and creates all this new land. The steam it makes is just incredible, huge white clouds of it... but, um, what were we talking about? Distress? Yeah, no. If I couldn't eat, I'd probably just be slightly irritated, maybe sigh a bit, and then just watch my show. It wouldn't be a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no choice but to eat at night once you start thinking about food?\n\nPatient: No choice? Um... well, I definitely have a choice. It's not like I'm being forced or anything. I make choices all the time. Like when I go to the grocery store, I'm always making choices. I usually go to the big Safeway on the corner of 5th and Main, because the produce is just so much better there. Though, last time I went, I spent like twenty minutes just standing in the cereal aisle trying to pick out a box of cornflakes. There are just so many brands now, it's overwhelming. Do you remember when they used to make those little variety packs with the tiny cardboard boxes? You could cut them open right down the middle and pour the milk straight into the box. My brother and I used to fight over the frosted ones when we went camping. He's always shouting about his \"crazy low prices.\" My uncle actually bought a truck from him once, a red pickup. It broke down two weeks later, right in the middle of an intersection. He had to get it towed all the way back to the dealership, and the tow truck driver was this really grumpy guy who complained the whole way about his sciatica. Um... no, I don't think so. It's not like a pressure cooker or anything. I'm not sitting there feeling like I'm going to burst if I don't have a sandwich. She used to make this amazing pot roast in it, with potatoes and carrots that got so soft they just melted in your mouth. She always grew her own carrots in the backyard, right next to the prize-winning tomatoes she was so proud of. She entered those tomatoes in the county fair every single year. The fair used to be so much better when I was a kid. They had this Ferris wheel that went so high you could see all the way to the water tower on the edge of town. There was an orchard down the road where you could pick your own... wait, what was the question? Internal pressure? Right, no. I don't feel pressured to eat. The tub is one of those old clawfoot ones, really deep. I love taking baths in it, especially with those lavender bath bombs my sister gets me for my birthday. She buys them from this little boutique downtown that smells so strongly of patchouli you can smell it halfway down the block. The crust is always perfectly crispy, and if you get it right when they open, it's still warm. I used to go there every Saturday morning when I was taking classes at the community college. Like, some weeks I barely touch my dinner, just move it around the plate. But then other nights, I'll just stand in the kitchen eating crackers and sweets after everyone's gone to bed. The kitchen is always so quiet at night, except for the hum of the refrigerator. It's an older model, one of those side-by-sides with the ice maker in the door. The ice maker actually broke last summer right in the middle of that huge heatwave we had. It was miserable. We had to buy bags of ice from the gas station on the corner. The guy who works the night shift there always wears these really bright, neon-colored shirts. He said his daughter bought them for him because he used to ride his bike to work and she wanted him to be visible to cars. Biking at night always seemed dangerous to me, especially since the streetlights in our neighborhood are so dim. The city said they were going to replace them with LED bulbs, but they only did half the street and then just stopped. It looks so strange, half bright white and half that weird orange glow. The orange glow kind of reminds me of Halloween, which used to be my favorite holiday until... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the eating. Yeah, it's just very unpredictable right now, the crackers and the sweets and not eating dinner. It's just all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat at night because you're feeling emotional or trying to deal with your feelings?\n\nPatient: Feeling emotional? Um... I don't know if I'd say I'm eating because of my feelings. It's more like... I don't know, maybe I'm just bored sometimes? Or I just see the cookies sitting there on the counter. I bought this really nice ceramic cookie jar at a garage sale a few weeks ago. It's shaped like a little owl, with big painted eyes. The lady who sold it to me said it belonged to her mother, who collected all sorts of owl things. She had owl figurines, owl salt and pepper shakers, even a clock that hooted on the hour instead of chiming. Clocks like that always kind of startle me, to be honest. My grandfather had a cuckoo clock that drove me crazy when I'd spend the night at his house. It was right outside the guest bedroom, and every hour, this little wooden bird would pop out and just... squawk. He brought it back from Germany when he was stationed there in the army. He used to tell the best stories about being in Europe, how different the food was and the architecture. He loved the old cathedrals with the stained glass windows. Stained glass is so beautiful when the light hits it just right, especially in the late afternoon when the sun is setting and it casts all those colors across the floor... um, wait.
